\title{Assumptions that were never used: the catalogue}

\section*{What this is}
Every one of the 645 survivors of a mechanical search across 19 areas of Mathlib: theorems whose stated algebraic setting is stronger than their own proof requires. Each entry gives the declaration, the module it lives in, the assumption as stated, and the weakest class at which the original proof still compiles.

The signature shown is what Lean prints for the theorem as the library has it, not our reconstruction of it.

\textbf{Two instruments.} All areas but CategoryTheory were swept with a hand-written table of 48 weakenings. That table names no categorical class, so CategoryTheory was swept with one derived from the library's own \texttt{{class \dots\ extends}} declarations. The two are not interchangeable and the areas are not comparable to each other.
\textbf{Two kinds of entry.} An entry marked $\ddagger$ is one whose weakened proof is \emph{not} the original proof: re-elaborated without the assumption, a tactic searched again and found another route, so the assumption was \emph{replaceable} rather than unused. An unmarked entry is the same proof term modulo the substituted class --- the assumption was never used at all. Of the entries that could be classified, 87.5\% are unused and 12.5\% replaceable. An entry marked ? could not be classified because the original declaration is \texttt{private} and cannot be named from outside its own file; those entries verify like any other and are findings, not failures.

\textbf{Nothing here is filtered for interest.} The search establishes that a proof survives a weaker hypothesis; it cannot establish that the weaker statement is worth having. Some entries are auxiliary lemmas whose conclusion barely involves the weakened structure, and they are listed alongside the rest because dropping them would claim a judgement the machine did not make. \emph{Depth is not worth.}
